People at work have started noticing, and I’m worried it’s becoming a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think so. I try not to make too big a deal of it, but most days I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day already feels lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I don’t really enjoy things the way I used to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like chores, or like items on a list I’m supposed to care about but can’t really get myself to start. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing that I’d say I really look forward to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… and now it all just feels like stuff on a checklist that I should care about. I can’t seem to care enough to actually start, if that’s… the kind of thing you mean.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. I just… I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that aren't yours or seem unreal to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure if this counts, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. I know that sounds strange. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard any other voices like that one, or has it only said things like 'You missed it, they know'?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just that same kind of thing… not a conversation or different voices, I don’t think. I try not to make too big a deal of it, but most days I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day already feels lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I don’t really enjoy things the way I used to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like chores, or like items on a list I’m supposed to care about but can’t really get myself to start. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing that I’d say I really look forward to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… and now it all just feels like stuff on a checklist that I should care about. I can’t seem to care enough to actually start, if that’s… the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, at least mostly. I used to be someone who planned hikes or went out taking photos, and I’d cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things I actually cared about. Now they just sort of sit there in my head like items on a checklist, and I can’t really make myself care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. It kind of started after my husband stopped working, when his company did the downsizing. I kept thinking it was just a stressful patch and I’d snap out of it, but… it’s been going on since then, more or less.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I have been worried. Maybe more than I should be. It’s mostly money and work.\n\nMy husband will show me the bank balance and tell me we’re okay, and my manager has said my job isn’t in danger, so I know that should be reassuring… but I can’t really make it land. I keep feeling like I’ve already ruined us financially somehow, and that at work they’re just quietly collecting proof that I’m incompetent and eventually everyone will know. I’m sorry, that probably sounds extreme. I just… I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that aren't yours or seem unreal to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure if this counts, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But sometimes when I open a spreadsheet or my email, I hear this… very clear voice, like it’s coming from just behind my laptop.\n\nIt’ll say something like, “You missed it, they know,” even when I’m the only person in the room. I know that sounds strange. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard any other voices like that one, or has it only said things like 'You missed it, they know'?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just that same kind of thing… not a conversation or different voices, I don’t think. It’s usually when I’m opening a spreadsheet or email, and it sounds really clear, like it’s just behind my laptop, saying, “You missed it, they know.”\n\nI know that probably sounds… not normal. It’s not all the time, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or thoughts that seem unreal to you besides the voice you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “unreal,” exactly… I mean, I understand other people don’t see it the way I do. My husband will show me the bank balance and say we’re okay, and my manager has told me my job isn’t in danger.\n\nBut I still feel… almost certain that I’ve already ruined us financially somehow. And at work, I keep thinking the firm is quietly building a case, like collecting mistakes to prove I’m incompetent and expose me.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic. I’m sorry. I’m trying not to overstate it, but it feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like making a plan or wanting to do something to myself.\n\nI mean, I get overwhelmed and I wish I could just not deal with everything for a while, but I don’t think I’ve had thoughts of death or suicide in the way you mean. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

I try not to make too big a deal of it, but most days I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day already feels lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I don’t really enjoy things the way I used to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. I used to be someone who planned hikes or went out taking photos, and I’d cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things I actually cared about. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. I kept thinking it was just a stressful patch and I’d snap out of it, but… it’s been going on since then, more or less.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I have been worried. Maybe more than I should be. It’s mostly money and work.\n\nMy husband will show me the bank balance and tell me we’re okay, and my manager has said my job isn’t in danger, so I know that should be reassuring… but I can’t really make it land. I used to be someone who planned hikes or went out taking photos, and I’d cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things I actually cared about. Now they just sort of sit there in my head like items on a checklist, and I can’t really make myself care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. It kind of started after my husband stopped working, when his company did the downsizing. I kept thinking it was just a stressful patch and I’d snap out of it, but… it’s been going on since then, more or less.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I have been worried. Maybe more than I should be. It’s mostly money and work.\n\nMy husband will show me the bank balance and tell me we’re okay, and my manager has said my job isn’t in danger, so I know that should be reassuring… but I can’t really make it land. I’m sorry, that probably sounds extreme. Like the day already feels lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I don’t really enjoy things the way I used to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like chores, or like items on a list I’m supposed to care about but can’t really get myself to start. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing that I’d say I really look forward to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… and now it all just feels like stuff on a checklist that I should care about. I can’t seem to care enough to actually start, if that’s… the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, at least mostly. I used to be someone who planned hikes or went out taking photos, and I’d cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things I actually cared about. Now they just sort of sit there in my head like items on a checklist, and I can’t really make myself care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. It kind of started after my husband stopped working, when his company did the downsizing. I kept thinking it was just a stressful patch and I’d snap out of it, but… it’s been going on since then, more or less.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I have been worried. Maybe more than I should be. It’s mostly money and work.\n\nMy husband will show me the bank balance and tell me we’re okay, and my manager has said my job isn’t in danger, so I know that should be reassuring… but I can’t really make it land. I keep feeling like I’ve already ruined us financially somehow, and that at work they’re just quietly collecting proof that I’m incompetent and eventually everyone will know. I’m sorry, that probably sounds extreme. I just… I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that aren't yours or seem unreal to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure if this counts, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But sometimes when I open a spreadsheet or my email, I hear this… very clear voice, like it’s coming from just behind my laptop.\n\nIt’ll say something like, “You missed it, they know,” even when I’m the only person in the room. I know that sounds strange. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard any other voices like that one, or has it only said things like 'You missed it, they know'?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just that same kind of thing… not a conversation or different voices, I don’t think. It’s usually when I’m opening a spreadsheet or email, and it sounds really clear, like it’s just behind my laptop, saying, “You missed it, they know.”\n\nI know that probably sounds… not normal. It’s not all the time, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or thoughts that seem unreal to you besides the voice you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “unreal,” exactly… I mean, I understand other people don’t see it the way I do. My husband will show me the bank balance and say we’re okay, and my manager has told me my job isn’t in danger.\n\nBut I still feel… almost certain that I’ve already ruined us financially somehow. And at work, I keep thinking the firm is quietly building a case, like collecting mistakes to prove I’m incompetent and expose me.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic. I’m sorry. I’m trying not to overstate it, but it feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. I… yes, I think so. I’m a little embarrassed to say it that way, because I know how it sounds.

It’s not constant, but sometimes when I open a spreadsheet or my email, I hear this clear voice, like it’s coming from just behind my laptop. It says, “You missed it, they know.” And there isn’t anyone else in the room.

I’ve been trying to tell myself it’s stress or my own thoughts, but it feels more like I’m hearing it. Does that count as what you mean?

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve been having a really hard time keeping up at work. I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but I feel like I’ve gotten… slowed down. Like I can sit staring at one audit workpaper for twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. People at work have started noticing, and I’m worried it’s becoming a problem.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I think so. I try not to make too big a deal of it, but most days I wake up with this heavy, kind of hollow feeling in my chest. Like the day already feels lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess I don’t really enjoy things the way I used to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like chores, or like items on a list I’m supposed to care about but can’t really get myself to start. I don’t know if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in?\n\nPatient: Not really… I mean, maybe little moments here and there, but nothing that I’d say I really look forward to. I used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… and now it all just feels like stuff on a checklist that I should care about. I can’t seem to care enough to actually start, if that’s… the kind of thing you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I have, at least mostly. I used to be someone who planned hikes or went out taking photos, and I’d cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things I actually cared about. Now they just sort of sit there in my head like items on a checklist, and I can’t really make myself care enough to start. I don’t mean to sound dramatic, but… yes, I guess that’s what it feels like.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. It kind of started after my husband stopped working, when his company did the downsizing. I kept thinking it was just a stressful patch and I’d snap out of it, but… it’s been going on since then, more or less.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or worried about things lately?\n\nPatient: I guess… yes, I have been worried. Maybe more than I should be. It’s mostly money and work.\n\nMy husband will show me the bank balance and tell me we’re okay, and my manager has said my job isn’t in danger, so I know that should be reassuring… but I can’t really make it land. I keep feeling like I’ve already ruined us financially somehow, and that at work they’re just quietly collecting proof that I’m incompetent and eventually everyone will know. I’m sorry, that probably sounds extreme. I just… I can’t shake it.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts or heard any voices that aren't yours or seem unreal to you?\n\nPatient: Um… I’m not sure if this counts, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is. But sometimes when I open a spreadsheet or my email, I hear this… very clear voice, like it’s coming from just behind my laptop.\n\nIt’ll say something like, “You missed it, they know,” even when I’m the only person in the room. I know that sounds strange. I’m sorry.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard any other voices like that one, or has it only said things like 'You missed it, they know'?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s just that same kind of thing… not a conversation or different voices, I don’t think. It’s usually when I’m opening a spreadsheet or email, and it sounds really clear, like it’s just behind my laptop, saying, “You missed it, they know.”\n\nI know that probably sounds… not normal. It’s not all the time, though.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other unusual perceptions or thoughts that seem unreal to you besides the voice you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s “unreal,” exactly… I mean, I understand other people don’t see it the way I do. My husband will show me the bank balance and say we’re okay, and my manager has told me my job isn’t in danger.\n\nBut I still feel… almost certain that I’ve already ruined us financially somehow. And at work, I keep thinking the firm is quietly building a case, like collecting mistakes to prove I’m incompetent and expose me.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic. I’m sorry. I’m trying not to overstate it, but it feels very real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I… no, I don’t think so. Not like making a plan or wanting to do something to myself.\n\nI mean, I get overwhelmed and I wish I could just not deal with everything for a while, but I don’t think I’ve had thoughts of death or suicide in the way you mean. Is that… is that okay to say?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your physical activity level or movement?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean… I don’t think I’m moving strangely, exactly, but I’ve gotten really slow.\n\nAt work I’ll be looking at one audit workpaper and just… sit there. Like twenty minutes can go by before I even move my hand to type something. I know that sounds bad. People have started noticing I’m not keeping up, so I guess it’s more than just being tired. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other hallucinations besides the voice you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there are others, no… not like seeing things or anything.\n\nIt’s mostly that same kind of voice. Sometimes when I open a spreadsheet or my email, I hear it very clearly, like it’s coming from just behind my laptop, saying, “You missed it, they know.” And there won’t be anyone else in the room.\n\nI know that sounds… I don’t know, alarming. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is, but it does feel very clear when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sadness or hollow feeling is most of the day or only some of the day?\n\nPatient: I think… most of the day, honestly. I was going to say it comes and goes, because I don’t want to make it sound dramatic, but most days I wake up with this heavy, hollow feeling in my chest, like the day is already lost before I’ve even gotten out of bed.\n\nIt can ease a little if I’m distracted, but it’s kind of there underneath. Is that what you mean by most of the day?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in almost all activities most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still technically could do those things, but I just don’t seem to care enough to start.\n\nI used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like items on a checklist, and even nice things feel like chores. Most of the day, it’s like there’s no pull toward any of it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I haven’t been weighing myself a lot, so I don’t want to guess too much. But my work skirts have been getting looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t been very appealing. I mostly eat because I know I’m supposed to, kind of forcing it down. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I kept telling myself I’m just tired or being inefficient, but it’s gotten noticeable.\n\nAt work, I can sit there staring at one audit workpaper for, like, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. It’s embarrassing to say that. And people have started noticing I’m not keeping up the way I normally do.\n\nSo… yes, I guess slower most of the day would fit?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voice or have those thoughts of being financially ruined or incompetent most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I hear it like someone standing in the room, exactly. But the thoughts are there a lot of the day, and they feel… very certain.\n\nEven when my husband shows me the bank balance and says we’re okay, I still feel like I’ve already ruined us financially somehow. Like twenty minutes can go by before I even move my hand to type something. I know that sounds bad. People have started noticing I’m not keeping up, so I guess it’s more than just being tired. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any other hallucinations besides the voice you mentioned?\n\nPatient: I don’t think there are others, no… not like seeing things or anything.\n\nIt’s mostly that same kind of voice. I’m not trying to make it bigger than it is, but it does feel very clear when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your sadness or hollow feeling is most of the day or only some of the day?\n\nPatient: I think… most of the day, honestly. I don’t want to overstate it, because I still technically could do those things, but I just don’t seem to care enough to start.\n\nI used to plan hikes, take photos, cook on Sundays, volunteer at the library… things like that. Now they just feel like items on a checklist, and even nice things feel like chores. Most of the day, it’s like there’s no pull toward any of it. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight recently?\n\nPatient: A little, I think… I haven’t been weighing myself a lot, so I don’t want to guess too much. But my work skirts have been getting looser, and I’m not trying to lose weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t been very appealing. I mostly eat because I know I’m supposed to, kind of forcing it down. Does that count as significant?\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving slower than usual most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I kept telling myself I’m just tired or being inefficient, but it’s gotten noticeable.\n\nAt work, I can sit there staring at one audit workpaper for, like, twenty minutes before I even move my hand to type anything. It’s embarrassing to say that. And people have started noticing I’m not keeping up the way I normally do.\n\nSo… yes, I guess slower most of the day would fit?\n\nDoctor: Do you hear the voice or have those thoughts of being financially ruined or incompetent most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I wouldn’t say I hear it like someone standing in the room, exactly. But the thoughts are there a lot of the day, and they feel… very certain.\n\nEven when my husband shows me the bank balance and says we’re okay, I still feel like I’ve already ruined us financially somehow. And when my manager tells me my job isn’t in danger, I can’t really believe it. I keep thinking the firm is quietly gathering evidence that I’m incompetent and they’re going to expose me.\n\nI know that probably sounds extreme. I’m sorry. I’m trying to answer it the right way.\n\nDoctor: How many months have you been feeling this heavy, hollow feeling in your chest?\n\nPatient: Um… I think it’s been about seven months now. Maybe since around when my husband stopped working after the downsizing.\n\nI kept thinking it was just stress and that I should handle it better, so I didn’t really mark the time at first. But… yes, about seven months.
